Yes… very much. I used to really look forward to my model railway, for example. I had all these little plans for it—where the track would go, tiny buildings, little details I’d spend hours fiddling with. It sounds silly, maybe, but it used to give me a real sense of enjoyment. Lately even opening a game on TV feels pointless. I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to, and that bothers me because those were things that used to feel like me.

Yes. Very much so. It’s not even the kind of tired where I can say, “Well, I did a lot today, so of course I’m worn out.” It happens when I’ve barely done anything.

Something as ordinary as carrying a basket of laundry upstairs feels like this huge undertaking, like my legs are full of sand or I’m moving through wet cement. And sorting the mail—just standing there, opening envelopes, putting things in piles—some days that feels like more than I can manage. I’ll look at it and think, “This should take five minutes,” but my body feels like it’s already used up everything it had.

And it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed, either. I can lie there for ages and still get up feeling like I haven’t actually rested. It’s frustrating, because these are such small things, things I shouldn’t even have to think about, but lately they feel like climbing a hill.

I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to, and that bothers me because those were things that used to feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t done much physically?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so. It’s not even the kind of tired where I can say, “Well, I did a lot today, so of course I’m worn out.” It happens when I’ve barely done anything.\n\nSomething as ordinary as carrying a basket of laundry upstairs feels like this huge undertaking, like my legs are full of sand or I’m moving through wet cement. I can lie there for ages and still get up feeling like I haven’t actually rested. It’s frustrating, because these are such small things, things I shouldn’t even have to think about, but lately they feel like climbing a hill.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced episodes like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 51 / not_in_workforce / graduate\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. I’m… not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone has, I suppose—times when I’ve been disappointed in myself or felt worn down—but I don’t know if I would call them the same as this.

I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I wish I had a neat answer, like “yes, there were two months here” or “no, it was continuous,” but when I try to look back, it all gets a bit blurred.

It’s not even the kind of tired where I can say, “Well, I did a lot today, so of course I’m worn out.” It happens when I’ve barely done anything.\n\nSomething as ordinary as carrying a basket of laundry upstairs feels like this huge undertaking, like my legs are full of sand or I’m moving through wet cement. And sorting the mail—just standing there, opening envelopes, putting things in piles—some days that feels like more than I can manage. I’ll look at it and think, “This should take five minutes,” but my body feels like it’s already used up everything it had.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed, either. I can lie there for ages and still get up feeling like I haven’t actually rested. It’s frustrating, because these are such small things, things I shouldn’t even have to think about, but lately they feel like climbing a hill.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced episodes like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone has, I suppose—times when I’ve been disappointed in myself or felt worn down—but I don’t know if I would call them the same as this.\n\nThis feels different in how much it has taken over, if that makes sense. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, this exact thing happened before.” But I also don’t want to say with absolute certainty that this is the first time I’ve ever felt bad, because life has had its difficult stretches. It’s just that this one feels heavier and harder to climb out of than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Between this current episode and any previous times you might have felt down or low, were there ever at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again—where you had your usual energy, interest in things, and weren’t struggling with these kinds of thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I wish I had a neat answer, like “yes, there were two months here” or “no, it was continuous,” but when I try to look back, it all gets a bit blurred.\n\nI can remember periods where I was probably functioning more like myself, at least on the outside. I’d get through things, talk to people, maybe keep up with some routines. But whether I really had my usual energy and interest back for a solid couple of months… I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. Some times I wasn’t struggling in the same obvious way. But I can’t confidently point to a clean break where everything lifted and I felt fully like myself again for months. I just don’t have that clear in my head.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

It’s just that this one feels heavier and harder to climb out of than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Between this current episode and any previous times you might have felt down or low, were there ever at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again—where you had your usual energy, interest in things, and weren’t struggling with these kinds of thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I wish I had a neat answer, like “yes, there were two months here” or “no, it was continuous,” but when I try to look back, it all gets a bit blurred.\n\nI can remember periods where I was probably functioning more like myself, at least on the outside. I’d get through things, talk to people, maybe keep up with some routines. But whether I really had my usual energy and interest back for a solid couple of months… I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. Some times I wasn’t struggling in the same obvious way. But I can’t confidently point to a clean break where everything lifted and I felt fully like myself again for months. I just don’t have that clear in my head.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall, including any better or worse stretches?\n\nPatient: Overall, I’d say it’s been about a month now. It kind of started around when my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI don’t know that I noticed it all at once on the exact day or anything, but looking back, that’s the point I keep coming back to. After she left, the house felt different, and I think I just… started slipping into this. There may have been slightly better days and worse days in there, but overall it’s been about a month of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way—losing interest in things and feeling down—for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think back as you ask that, and it’s hard to separate ordinary bad periods from something like this.\n\nI can remember being low before, or losing some enthusiasm for things for a while, especially during stressful times, but whether it was more than two weeks and really the same kind of down, uninterested feeling… I’m not confident saying that. I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because I don’t want to give you a wrong answer just because my memory is fuzzy.

There have been times in my life when I’ve been down, definitely, and times when I wasn’t as interested in things as I usually would be. But whether that happened more than once, for more than a couple of weeks each time, and then had these obvious normal stretches in between where I was really back to myself… I just can’t say that with confidence.

It’s more like scattered rough patches, and I don’t know how long they lasted or how complete the “getting better” was afterward. So I guess the honest answer is that I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. I’ve been trying to think back as you ask that, and it’s hard to separate ordinary bad periods from something like this.\n\nI can remember being low before, or losing some enthusiasm for things for a while, especially during stressful times, but whether it was more than two weeks and really the same kind of down, uninterested feeling… I’m not confident saying that. I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall having more than one time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, with clear periods of normal mood in between those times?\n\nPatient: I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because I don’t want to give you a wrong answer just because my memory is fuzzy.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve been down, definitely, and times when I wasn’t as interested in things as I usually would be. I don’t really know that I can say yes to that. I’ve had hard stretches before, but when you put it that specifically—more than one time, down or not interested in things, and for more than two weeks—I can’t clearly identify separate periods like that.

I’d get through things, talk to people, maybe keep up with some routines. But whether I really had my usual energy and interest back for a solid couple of months… I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. Some times I wasn’t struggling in the same obvious way. But I can’t confidently point to a clean break where everything lifted and I felt fully like myself again for months. I just don’t have that clear in my head.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall, including any better or worse stretches?\n\nPatient: Overall, I’d say it’s been about a month now. It kind of started around when my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI don’t know that I noticed it all at once on the exact day or anything, but looking back, that’s the point I keep coming back to. After she left, the house felt different, and I think I just… started slipping into this. There may have been slightly better days and worse days in there, but overall it’s been about a month of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way—losing interest in things and feeling down—for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think back as you ask that, and it’s hard to separate ordinary bad periods from something like this.\n\nI can remember being low before, or losing some enthusiasm for things for a while, especially during stressful times, but whether it was more than two weeks and really the same kind of down, uninterested feeling… I’m not confident saying that. I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall having more than one time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, with clear periods of normal mood in between those times?\n\nPatient: I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because I don’t want to give you a wrong answer just because my memory is fuzzy.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve been down, definitely, and times when I wasn’t as interested in things as I usually would be. But whether that happened more than once, for more than a couple of weeks each time, and then had these obvious normal stretches in between where I was really back to myself… I just can’t say that with confidence.\n\nWhen I look back, it’s not organized in my mind like separate episodes. It’s more like scattered rough patches, and I don’t know how long they lasted or how complete the “getting better” was afterward. So I guess the honest answer is that I don’t know. This current time is much clearer to me than anything in the past.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one time in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know that I can say yes to that. I’ve had hard stretches before, but when you put it that specifically—more than one time, down or not interested in things, and for more than two weeks—I can’t clearly identify separate periods like that.\n\nIt’s not that my life has been perfectly even or anything. I can remember times when I felt low or just kind of disconnected, but I don’t remember them well enough to say how long they lasted, or whether they were really the same as what’s been happening now. They kind of blur together in hindsight.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: I’m not sure. We’d watch together, and I used to get into it, even just having the game on felt like something to look forward to. Lately even opening a game on TV feels pointless. I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to, and that bothers me because those were things that used to feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t done much physically?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so. It’s not even the kind of tired where I can say, “Well, I did a lot today, so of course I’m worn out.” It happens when I’ve barely done anything.\n\nSomething as ordinary as carrying a basket of laundry upstairs feels like this huge undertaking, like my legs are full of sand or I’m moving through wet cement. And sorting the mail—just standing there, opening envelopes, putting things in piles—some days that feels like more than I can manage. I’ll look at it and think, “This should take five minutes,” but my body feels like it’s already used up everything it had.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed, either. I can lie there for ages and still get up feeling like I haven’t actually rested. It’s frustrating, because these are such small things, things I shouldn’t even have to think about, but lately they feel like climbing a hill.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced episodes like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone has, I suppose—times when I’ve been disappointed in myself or felt worn down—but I don’t know if I would call them the same as this.\n\nThis feels different in how much it has taken over, if that makes sense. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, this exact thing happened before.” But I also don’t want to say with absolute certainty that this is the first time I’ve ever felt bad, because life has had its difficult stretches. It’s just that this one feels heavier and harder to climb out of than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Between this current episode and any previous times you might have felt down or low, were there ever at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again—where you had your usual energy, interest in things, and weren’t struggling with these kinds of thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I wish I had a neat answer, like “yes, there were two months here” or “no, it was continuous,” but when I try to look back, it all gets a bit blurred.\n\nI can remember periods where I was probably functioning more like myself, at least on the outside. I’d get through things, talk to people, maybe keep up with some routines. But whether I really had my usual energy and interest back for a solid couple of months… I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. Some times I wasn’t struggling in the same obvious way. But I can’t confidently point to a clean break where everything lifted and I felt fully like myself again for months. I just don’t have that clear in my head.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall, including any better or worse stretches?\n\nPatient: Overall, I’d say it’s been about a month now. It kind of started around when my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI don’t know that I noticed it all at once on the exact day or anything, but looking back, that’s the point I keep coming back to. After she left, the house felt different, and I think I just… started slipping into this. There may have been slightly better days and worse days in there, but overall it’s been about a month of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way—losing interest in things and feeling down—for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think back as you ask that, and it’s hard to separate ordinary bad periods from something like this.\n\nI can remember being low before, or losing some enthusiasm for things for a while, especially during stressful times, but whether it was more than two weeks and really the same kind of down, uninterested feeling… I’m not confident saying that. I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall having more than one time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, with clear periods of normal mood in between those times?\n\nPatient: I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because I don’t want to give you a wrong answer just because my memory is fuzzy.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve been down, definitely, and times when I wasn’t as interested in things as I usually would be. But whether that happened more than once, for more than a couple of weeks each time, and then had these obvious normal stretches in between where I was really back to myself… I just can’t say that with confidence.\n\nWhen I look back, it’s not organized in my mind like separate episodes. It’s more like scattered rough patches, and I don’t know how long they lasted or how complete the “getting better” was afterward. So I guess the honest answer is that I don’t know. This current time is much clearer to me than anything in the past.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one time in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know that I can say yes to that. I’ve had hard stretches before, but when you put it that specifically—more than one time, down or not interested in things, and for more than two weeks—I can’t clearly identify separate periods like that.\n\nIt’s not that my life has been perfectly even or anything. I can remember times when I felt low or just kind of disconnected, but I don’t remember them well enough to say how long they lasted, or whether they were really the same as what’s been happening now. They kind of blur together in hindsight.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: I’m not sure. I can’t confidently say I’ve had more than one episode like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know I’m probably not being very helpful, but I really can’t say for sure. I’ve been trying to sort through it in my head, and there were definitely times in the past where I was low or less interested in things, but I don’t know if any of them lasted more than two weeks straight.\n\nThat’s the part I get stuck on—“in a row” and whether it was really the same kind of feeling as now. Looking back, it’s all kind of blurry. I don’t have a clear memory of one specific past period where I can say, yes, that was definitely more than two weeks of feeling down or losing interest. So the most honest answer is that I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I understand what you’re asking, and I’m trying not to be evasive, but I still don’t think I can give you a clean answer.

I’m still not able to really recall one clearly. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question because you’ve asked it a few different ways, but I’m honestly trying to check my memory and not just give you an answer because it would be simpler.

And sorting the mail—just standing there, opening envelopes, putting things in piles—some days that feels like more than I can manage. I’ll look at it and think, “This should take five minutes,” but my body feels like it’s already used up everything it had.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed, either. I can lie there for ages and still get up feeling like I haven’t actually rested. It’s frustrating, because these are such small things, things I shouldn’t even have to think about, but lately they feel like climbing a hill.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced episodes like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone has, I suppose—times when I’ve been disappointed in myself or felt worn down—but I don’t know if I would call them the same as this.\n\nThis feels different in how much it has taken over, if that makes sense. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, this exact thing happened before.” But I also don’t want to say with absolute certainty that this is the first time I’ve ever felt bad, because life has had its difficult stretches. It’s just that this one feels heavier and harder to climb out of than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Between this current episode and any previous times you might have felt down or low, were there ever at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again—where you had your usual energy, interest in things, and weren’t struggling with these kinds of thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I wish I had a neat answer, like “yes, there were two months here” or “no, it was continuous,” but when I try to look back, it all gets a bit blurred.\n\nI can remember periods where I was probably functioning more like myself, at least on the outside. I’d get through things, talk to people, maybe keep up with some routines. But whether I really had my usual energy and interest back for a solid couple of months… I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. Some times I wasn’t struggling in the same obvious way. But I can’t confidently point to a clean break where everything lifted and I felt fully like myself again for months. I just don’t have that clear in my head.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall, including any better or worse stretches?\n\nPatient: Overall, I’d say it’s been about a month now. It kind of started around when my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI don’t know that I noticed it all at once on the exact day or anything, but looking back, that’s the point I keep coming back to. After she left, the house felt different, and I think I just… started slipping into this. There may have been slightly better days and worse days in there, but overall it’s been about a month of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way—losing interest in things and feeling down—for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think back as you ask that, and it’s hard to separate ordinary bad periods from something like this.\n\nI can remember being low before, or losing some enthusiasm for things for a while, especially during stressful times, but whether it was more than two weeks and really the same kind of down, uninterested feeling… I’m not confident saying that. I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall having more than one time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, with clear periods of normal mood in between those times?\n\nPatient: I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because I don’t want to give you a wrong answer just because my memory is fuzzy.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve been down, definitely, and times when I wasn’t as interested in things as I usually would be. But whether that happened more than once, for more than a couple of weeks each time, and then had these obvious normal stretches in between where I was really back to myself… I just can’t say that with confidence.\n\nWhen I look back, it’s not organized in my mind like separate episodes. It’s more like scattered rough patches, and I don’t know how long they lasted or how complete the “getting better” was afterward. So I guess the honest answer is that I don’t know. This current time is much clearer to me than anything in the past.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one time in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know that I can say yes to that. I’ve had hard stretches before, but when you put it that specifically—more than one time, down or not interested in things, and for more than two weeks—I can’t clearly identify separate periods like that.\n\nIt’s not that my life has been perfectly even or anything. I can remember times when I felt low or just kind of disconnected, but I don’t remember them well enough to say how long they lasted, or whether they were really the same as what’s been happening now. They kind of blur together in hindsight.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: I’m not sure. I can’t confidently say I’ve had more than one episode like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know I’m probably not being very helpful, but I really can’t say for sure. I’ve been trying to sort through it in my head, and there were definitely times in the past where I was low or less interested in things, but I don’t know if any of them lasted more than two weeks straight.\n\nThat’s the part I get stuck on—“in a row” and whether it was really the same kind of feeling as now. Looking back, it’s all kind of blurry. I don’t have a clear memory of one specific past period where I can say, yes, that was definitely more than two weeks of feeling down or losing interest. So the most honest answer is that I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any time in the past when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t quite as bad as what you’re experiencing now?\n\nPatient: I understand what you’re asking, and I’m trying not to be evasive, but I still don’t think I can give you a clean answer.\n\nIf you mean any stretch at all where I felt lower than usual or wasn’t enjoying things the way I normally would, then yes, I can remember having rough times in the past. But when you add the part about it lasting more than two weeks in a row, that’s where I honestly get uncertain. I don’t have a clear memory of, like, “this started here and ended there,” or “that was definitely two or three weeks straight.”\n\nA lot of it feels kind of blurry when I look back. I can remember the general feeling of being down at different points, but not the duration well enough to say it meets what you’re asking. So even if it wasn’t as bad as now, I can’t confidently point to a past period and say, yes, that definitely happened for more than two weeks continuously.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a low mood or loss of interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks without a break, even if it wasn’t quite as severe as what you’re going through now?\n\nPatient: I’m still not able to really recall one clearly. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question because you’ve asked it a few different ways, but I’m honestly trying to check my memory and not just give you an answer because it would be simpler.\n\nBefore this, I can think of times when I was kind of low, or quieter, or less engaged with things, but I can’t pin down whether it went on for more than two weeks without any real break. That “without a break” part is hard for me, because looking back I don’t remember it in neat stretches. It’s more like impressions—periods where I wasn’t myself—but I don’t know if it was continuous or how long it lasted.\n\nSo I guess the answer is: I can’t recall a definite time before now that fits that. There may have been rough patches, but I can’t say confidently that there was a more-than-two-week stretch like you’re describing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

So the most honest answer is that I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any time in the past when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t quite as bad as what you’re experiencing now?\n\nPatient: I understand what you’re asking, and I’m trying not to be evasive, but I still don’t think I can give you a clean answer.\n\nIf you mean any stretch at all where I felt lower than usual or wasn’t enjoying things the way I normally would, then yes, I can remember having rough times in the past. But when you add the part about it lasting more than two weeks in a row, that’s where I honestly get uncertain. I don’t have a clear memory of, like, “this started here and ended there,” or “that was definitely two or three weeks straight.”\n\nA lot of it feels kind of blurry when I look back. I can remember the general feeling of being down at different points, but not the duration well enough to say it meets what you’re asking. So even if it wasn’t as bad as now, I can’t confidently point to a past period and say, yes, that definitely happened for more than two weeks continuously.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a low mood or loss of interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks without a break, even if it wasn’t quite as severe as what you’re going through now?\n\nPatient: I’m still not able to really recall one clearly. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question because you’ve asked it a few different ways, but I’m honestly trying to check my memory and not just give you an answer because it would be simpler.\n\nBefore this, I can think of times when I was kind of low, or quieter, or less engaged with things, but I can’t pin down whether it went on for more than two weeks without any real break. That “without a break” part is hard for me, because looking back I don’t remember it in neat stretches. It’s more like impressions—periods where I wasn’t myself—but I don’t know if it was continuous or how long it lasted.\n\nSo I guess the answer is: I can’t recall a definite time before now that fits that. There may have been rough patches, but I can’t say confidently that there was a more-than-two-week stretch like you’re describing.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you recall a time before this current episode when you felt down or lost interest in things for a period that lasted more than two weeks without significantly improving in between?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 51 / not_in_workforce / graduate\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wish I could give you a better answer, but no, not in a way I can really stand behind.

I’ve been trying to search back through it while you’re asking, and I can remember times where I wasn’t exactly cheerful or where I pulled back from things a bit, but I can’t clearly remember a period before this where it stayed that way for more than two weeks and didn’t improve in between. That’s the part that makes me hesitate. I don’t want to say yes just because there were rough patches, because I don’t actually know if they were continuous like that.

It’s frustrating, honestly, because my memory of those times isn’t organized. It’s not like I can look back and see a clean block of time where I can say, “Yes, those two or three weeks were definitely like this.” It’s more vague than that. So I’d have to say I can’t recall a definite earlier period that fits what you’re asking.

I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall having more than one time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, with clear periods of normal mood in between those times?\n\nPatient: I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I used to really look forward to my model railway, for example. I had all these little plans for it—where the track would go, tiny buildings, little details I’d spend hours fiddling with. It sounds silly, maybe, but it used to give me a real sense of enjoyment. Now the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table, and I’ll look at them and think, “I should do something with that,” but I just… don’t feel anything for it.\n\nAnd Cubs games with my daughter, that was another thing. We’d watch together, and I used to get into it, even just having the game on felt like something to look forward to. Lately even opening a game on TV feels pointless. I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to, and that bothers me because those were things that used to feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t done much physically?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so. It’s not even the kind of tired where I can say, “Well, I did a lot today, so of course I’m worn out.” It happens when I’ve barely done anything.\n\nSomething as ordinary as carrying a basket of laundry upstairs feels like this huge undertaking, like my legs are full of sand or I’m moving through wet cement. And sorting the mail—just standing there, opening envelopes, putting things in piles—some days that feels like more than I can manage. I’ll look at it and think, “This should take five minutes,” but my body feels like it’s already used up everything it had.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed, either. I can lie there for ages and still get up feeling like I haven’t actually rested. It’s frustrating, because these are such small things, things I shouldn’t even have to think about, but lately they feel like climbing a hill.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced episodes like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone has, I suppose—times when I’ve been disappointed in myself or felt worn down—but I don’t know if I would call them the same as this.\n\nThis feels different in how much it has taken over, if that makes sense. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, this exact thing happened before.” But I also don’t want to say with absolute certainty that this is the first time I’ve ever felt bad, because life has had its difficult stretches. It’s just that this one feels heavier and harder to climb out of than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Between this current episode and any previous times you might have felt down or low, were there ever at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again—where you had your usual energy, interest in things, and weren’t struggling with these kinds of thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I wish I had a neat answer, like “yes, there were two months here” or “no, it was continuous,” but when I try to look back, it all gets a bit blurred.\n\nI can remember periods where I was probably functioning more like myself, at least on the outside. I’d get through things, talk to people, maybe keep up with some routines. But whether I really had my usual energy and interest back for a solid couple of months… I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. Some times I wasn’t struggling in the same obvious way. But I can’t confidently point to a clean break where everything lifted and I felt fully like myself again for months. I just don’t have that clear in my head.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall, including any better or worse stretches?\n\nPatient: Overall, I’d say it’s been about a month now. It kind of started around when my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI don’t know that I noticed it all at once on the exact day or anything, but looking back, that’s the point I keep coming back to. After she left, the house felt different, and I think I just… started slipping into this. There may have been slightly better days and worse days in there, but overall it’s been about a month of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way—losing interest in things and feeling down—for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think back as you ask that, and it’s hard to separate ordinary bad periods from something like this.\n\nI can remember being low before, or losing some enthusiasm for things for a while, especially during stressful times, but whether it was more than two weeks and really the same kind of down, uninterested feeling… I’m not confident saying that. I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall having more than one time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, with clear periods of normal mood in between those times?\n\nPatient: I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because I don’t want to give you a wrong answer just because my memory is fuzzy.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve been down, definitely, and times when I wasn’t as interested in things as I usually would be. But whether that happened more than once, for more than a couple of weeks each time, and then had these obvious normal stretches in between where I was really back to myself… I just can’t say that with confidence.\n\nWhen I look back, it’s not organized in my mind like separate episodes. It’s more like scattered rough patches, and I don’t know how long they lasted or how complete the “getting better” was afterward. So I guess the honest answer is that I don’t know. This current time is much clearer to me than anything in the past.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one time in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know that I can say yes to that. I’ve had hard stretches before, but when you put it that specifically—more than one time, down or not interested in things, and for more than two weeks—I can’t clearly identify separate periods like that.\n\nIt’s not that my life has been perfectly even or anything. I can remember times when I felt low or just kind of disconnected, but I don’t remember them well enough to say how long they lasted, or whether they were really the same as what’s been happening now. They kind of blur together in hindsight.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: I’m not sure. I can’t confidently say I’ve had more than one episode like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know I’m probably not being very helpful, but I really can’t say for sure. I’ve been trying to sort through it in my head, and there were definitely times in the past where I was low or less interested in things, but I don’t know if any of them lasted more than two weeks straight.\n\nThat’s the part I get stuck on—“in a row” and whether it was really the same kind of feeling as now. Looking back, it’s all kind of blurry. I don’t have a clear memory of one specific past period where I can say, yes, that was definitely more than two weeks of feeling down or losing interest. So the most honest answer is that I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any time in the past when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t quite as bad as what you’re experiencing now?\n\nPatient: I understand what you’re asking, and I’m trying not to be evasive, but I still don’t think I can give you a clean answer.\n\nIf you mean any stretch at all where I felt lower than usual or wasn’t enjoying things the way I normally would, then yes, I can remember having rough times in the past. But when you add the part about it lasting more than two weeks in a row, that’s where I honestly get uncertain. I don’t have a clear memory of, like, “this started here and ended there,” or “that was definitely two or three weeks straight.”\n\nA lot of it feels kind of blurry when I look back. I can remember the general feeling of being down at different points, but not the duration well enough to say it meets what you’re asking. So even if it wasn’t as bad as now, I can’t confidently point to a past period and say, yes, that definitely happened for more than two weeks continuously.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a low mood or loss of interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks without a break, even if it wasn’t quite as severe as what you’re going through now?\n\nPatient: I’m still not able to really recall one clearly. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question because you’ve asked it a few different ways, but I’m honestly trying to check my memory and not just give you an answer because it would be simpler.\n\nBefore this, I can think of times when I was kind of low, or quieter, or less engaged with things, but I can’t pin down whether it went on for more than two weeks without any real break. That “without a break” part is hard for me, because looking back I don’t remember it in neat stretches. It’s more like impressions—periods where I wasn’t myself—but I don’t know if it was continuous or how long it lasted.\n\nSo I guess the answer is: I can’t recall a definite time before now that fits that. There may have been rough patches, but I can’t say confidently that there was a more-than-two-week stretch like you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you felt down or lost interest in things for a period that lasted more than two weeks without significantly improving in between?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a better answer, but no, not in a way I can really stand behind.\n\nI’ve been trying to search back through it while you’re asking, and I can remember times where I wasn’t exactly cheerful or where I pulled back from things a bit, but I can’t clearly remember a period before this where it stayed that way for more than two weeks and didn’t improve in between. That’s the part that makes me hesitate. I don’t want to say yes just because there were rough patches, because I don’t actually know if they were continuous like that.\n\nIt’s frustrating, honestly, because my memory of those times isn’t organized. It’s not like I can look back and see a clean block of time where I can say, “Yes, those two or three weeks were definitely like this.” It’s more vague than that. So I’d have to say I can’t recall a definite earlier period that fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks without getting significantly better during that time?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably giving you the least useful answer, but I still can’t really recall that clearly.\n\nI can remember being down at different points before this, or feeling like I wasn’t as interested in things as I should have been, but when you ask whether it lasted more than two weeks and didn’t get significantly better in between, I just don’t have a solid enough memory to say yes. It’s not that I’m saying it definitely never happened. It’s more that I can’t separate it out in my mind well enough.\n\nThose past times feel more like vague patches than something with a clear beginning and end. I don’t want to exaggerate it or accidentally turn a rough few days into something longer just because it seems relevant now. So, as far as I can honestly say, I can’t recall a definite period before this one that fits that description.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks and didn’t get significantly better during that time, even if it wasn’t exactly the same as what you’re going through now?\n\nPatient: I know we’ve circled around this a few times, and I’m sorry if I’m making it harder than it needs to be, but I really don’t have a clear example to give you.\n\nWhen I try to think back, I can remember stretches where I was not doing great emotionally, or where I was less interested in things, but I can’t honestly say, “Yes, that lasted more than two weeks and didn’t let up.” My memory of it just isn’t that precise. It’s more like I remember the general feeling of certain periods, not the exact duration or whether there were better days in the middle.\n\nSo I don’t want to say no as if I’m completely certain it never happened, because I’m not sure I can know that. But I also can’t say yes in a reliable way. I can’t recall a definite earlier period before this one that fits what you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I just… I can’t get away from this feeling that I’ve failed, especially as a father, and as a useful person in general. It’s like it keeps circling around in my head, over and over, and I start thinking I don’t really deserve much of a future. I know that probably sounds heavy to say right at the start, but that’s honestly what’s been bringing me here. It’s not just a passing thought—it feels like it has settled in and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Yes… very much. I used to really look forward to my model railway, for example. I had all these little plans for it—where the track would go, tiny buildings, little details I’d spend hours fiddling with. It sounds silly, maybe, but it used to give me a real sense of enjoyment. Now the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table, and I’ll look at them and think, “I should do something with that,” but I just… don’t feel anything for it.\n\nAnd Cubs games with my daughter, that was another thing. We’d watch together, and I used to get into it, even just having the game on felt like something to look forward to. Lately even opening a game on TV feels pointless. I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to, and that bothers me because those were things that used to feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t done much physically?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so. It’s not even the kind of tired where I can say, “Well, I did a lot today, so of course I’m worn out.” It happens when I’ve barely done anything.\n\nSomething as ordinary as carrying a basket of laundry upstairs feels like this huge undertaking, like my legs are full of sand or I’m moving through wet cement. And sorting the mail—just standing there, opening envelopes, putting things in piles—some days that feels like more than I can manage. I’ll look at it and think, “This should take five minutes,” but my body feels like it’s already used up everything it had.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed, either. I can lie there for ages and still get up feeling like I haven’t actually rested. It’s frustrating, because these are such small things, things I shouldn’t even have to think about, but lately they feel like climbing a hill.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced episodes like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone has, I suppose—times when I’ve been disappointed in myself or felt worn down—but I don’t know if I would call them the same as this.\n\nThis feels different in how much it has taken over, if that makes sense. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, this exact thing happened before.” But I also don’t want to say with absolute certainty that this is the first time I’ve ever felt bad, because life has had its difficult stretches. It’s just that this one feels heavier and harder to climb out of than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Between this current episode and any previous times you might have felt down or low, were there ever at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again—where you had your usual energy, interest in things, and weren’t struggling with these kinds of thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I wish I had a neat answer, like “yes, there were two months here” or “no, it was continuous,” but when I try to look back, it all gets a bit blurred.\n\nI can remember periods where I was probably functioning more like myself, at least on the outside. I’d get through things, talk to people, maybe keep up with some routines. But whether I really had my usual energy and interest back for a solid couple of months… I don’t know. I’m hesitant to say yes, because right now it’s hard to remember what “normal” even felt like in a precise way.\n\nIt’s more that there were better stretches and worse stretches, I think. Some times I wasn’t struggling in the same obvious way. But I can’t confidently point to a clean break where everything lifted and I felt fully like myself again for months. I just don’t have that clear in my head.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall, including any better or worse stretches?\n\nPatient: Overall, I’d say it’s been about a month now. It kind of started around when my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI don’t know that I noticed it all at once on the exact day or anything, but looking back, that’s the point I keep coming back to. After she left, the house felt different, and I think I just… started slipping into this. There may have been slightly better days and worse days in there, but overall it’s been about a month of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way—losing interest in things and feeling down—for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think back as you ask that, and it’s hard to separate ordinary bad periods from something like this.\n\nI can remember being low before, or losing some enthusiasm for things for a while, especially during stressful times, but whether it was more than two weeks and really the same kind of down, uninterested feeling… I’m not confident saying that. I don’t want to overstate it and say yes if it was just a rough patch, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m completely sure.\n\nThis current period is the one that stands out clearly to me. The past is more blurry—there were times I wasn’t great, but I can’t pin down whether they lasted that long or had the same quality.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall having more than one time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, with clear periods of normal mood in between those times?\n\nPatient: I can’t really recall that clearly, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because I don’t want to give you a wrong answer just because my memory is fuzzy.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve been down, definitely, and times when I wasn’t as interested in things as I usually would be. But whether that happened more than once, for more than a couple of weeks each time, and then had these obvious normal stretches in between where I was really back to myself… I just can’t say that with confidence.\n\nWhen I look back, it’s not organized in my mind like separate episodes. It’s more like scattered rough patches, and I don’t know how long they lasted or how complete the “getting better” was afterward. So I guess the honest answer is that I don’t know. This current time is much clearer to me than anything in the past.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one time in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know that I can say yes to that. I’ve had hard stretches before, but when you put it that specifically—more than one time, down or not interested in things, and for more than two weeks—I can’t clearly identify separate periods like that.\n\nIt’s not that my life has been perfectly even or anything. I can remember times when I felt low or just kind of disconnected, but I don’t remember them well enough to say how long they lasted, or whether they were really the same as what’s been happening now. They kind of blur together in hindsight.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: I’m not sure. I can’t confidently say I’ve had more than one episode like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know I’m probably not being very helpful, but I really can’t say for sure. I’ve been trying to sort through it in my head, and there were definitely times in the past where I was low or less interested in things, but I don’t know if any of them lasted more than two weeks straight.\n\nThat’s the part I get stuck on—“in a row” and whether it was really the same kind of feeling as now. Looking back, it’s all kind of blurry. I don’t have a clear memory of one specific past period where I can say, yes, that was definitely more than two weeks of feeling down or losing interest. So the most honest answer is that I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any time in the past when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t quite as bad as what you’re experiencing now?\n\nPatient: I understand what you’re asking, and I’m trying not to be evasive, but I still don’t think I can give you a clean answer.\n\nIf you mean any stretch at all where I felt lower than usual or wasn’t enjoying things the way I normally would, then yes, I can remember having rough times in the past. But when you add the part about it lasting more than two weeks in a row, that’s where I honestly get uncertain. I don’t have a clear memory of, like, “this started here and ended there,” or “that was definitely two or three weeks straight.”\n\nA lot of it feels kind of blurry when I look back. I can remember the general feeling of being down at different points, but not the duration well enough to say it meets what you’re asking. So even if it wasn’t as bad as now, I can’t confidently point to a past period and say, yes, that definitely happened for more than two weeks continuously.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a low mood or loss of interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks without a break, even if it wasn’t quite as severe as what you’re going through now?\n\nPatient: I’m still not able to really recall one clearly. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question because you’ve asked it a few different ways, but I’m honestly trying to check my memory and not just give you an answer because it would be simpler.\n\nBefore this, I can think of times when I was kind of low, or quieter, or less engaged with things, but I can’t pin down whether it went on for more than two weeks without any real break. That “without a break” part is hard for me, because looking back I don’t remember it in neat stretches. It’s more like impressions—periods where I wasn’t myself—but I don’t know if it was continuous or how long it lasted.\n\nSo I guess the answer is: I can’t recall a definite time before now that fits that. There may have been rough patches, but I can’t say confidently that there was a more-than-two-week stretch like you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you felt down or lost interest in things for a period that lasted more than two weeks without significantly improving in between?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a better answer, but no, not in a way I can really stand behind.\n\nI’ve been trying to search back through it while you’re asking, and I can remember times where I wasn’t exactly cheerful or where I pulled back from things a bit, but I can’t clearly remember a period before this where it stayed that way for more than two weeks and didn’t improve in between. That’s the part that makes me hesitate. I don’t want to say yes just because there were rough patches, because I don’t actually know if they were continuous like that.\n\nIt’s frustrating, honestly, because my memory of those times isn’t organized. It’s not like I can look back and see a clean block of time where I can say, “Yes, those two or three weeks were definitely like this.” It’s more vague than that. So I’d have to say I can’t recall a definite earlier period that fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks without getting significantly better during that time?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably giving you the least useful answer, but I still can’t really recall that clearly.\n\nI can remember being down at different points before this, or feeling like I wasn’t as interested in things as I should have been, but when you ask whether it lasted more than two weeks and didn’t get significantly better in between, I just don’t have a solid enough memory to say yes. It’s not that I’m saying it definitely never happened. It’s more that I can’t separate it out in my mind well enough.\n\nThose past times feel more like vague patches than something with a clear beginning and end. I don’t want to exaggerate it or accidentally turn a rough few days into something longer just because it seems relevant now. So, as far as I can honestly say, I can’t recall a definite period before this one that fits that description.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you had a period of feeling down or losing interest in things that lasted for more than two weeks and didn’t get significantly better during that time, even if it wasn’t exactly the same as what you’re going through now?\n\nPatient: I know we’ve circled around this a few times, and I’m sorry if I’m making it harder than it needs to be, but I really don’t have a clear example to give you.\n\nWhen I try to think back, I can remember stretches where I was not doing great emotionally, or where I was less interested in things, but I can’t honestly say, “Yes, that lasted more than two weeks and didn’t let up.” My memory of it just isn’t that precise. It’s more like I remember the general feeling of certain periods, not the exact duration or whether there were better days in the middle.\n\nSo I don’t want to say no as if I’m completely certain it never happened, because I’m not sure I can know that. But I also can’t say yes in a reliable way. I can’t recall a definite earlier period before this one that fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time before this current episode when you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m still not coming up with a clear time like that, no.\n\nI mean, “most of the day, nearly every day” makes it even harder for me to say yes, because that’s pretty specific. I can remember periods before where I was low or kind of withdrawn, but I can’t honestly tell you that it was most of the day, almost every day, for more than two weeks. I just don’t have that clear a memory of it.\n\nIt’s possible there were times that looked like that from the outside, or that I’m not remembering accurately, but I don’t want to fill in the gaps just because you’re asking. So the honest answer is that I can’t recall a definite earlier period that fits that description.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, can you recall a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can give you a definite yes to that. I’ve been trying to sort of scan back through my memory while you’re asking, and it’s not coming up in a clean way.\n\nThere were times before this where I was down, or where I wasn’t really enjoying things the way I normally would, but I can’t honestly say it was most of the day, nearly every day, for more than two weeks. That part matters, I know, and I don’t want to just assume it because it sounds similar.\n\nMy memory of those earlier periods is kind of blurry. I can remember the general mood of them, but not the day-to-day pattern well enough to say, “Yes, that definitely happened.” So I guess the most accurate answer is: I’m not sure, and I can’t recall a specific time before this current episode that clearly fits that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
